<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>H</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> be the index of the first unsatisfiable prefix in random <i>k</i>-SAT on <i>n</i> variables, with independent uniformly signed clauses using <i>k</i> distinct variables and sampled with replacement. For every fixed <i>k</i> ≥ 4, we prove $`\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\nolimits (H_n)=\Theta_k(n)`$. For <i>k</i> = 3, the variance is bounded below by a positive multiple of <i>n</i> and above by a constant multiple of $`n\log n`$; the companion paper on random 3-SAT sharpens this to $`\Theta(n)`$. The same upper bounds proved here hold after clipping at any fixed positive multiple of <i>n</i>.

- **claim-variance-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-variance)): Fix an integer $k\ge3$, and write $$\ell_k(n)=\begin{cases}\log(en),&k=3,\\1,&k\ge4,\end{cases} \qquad U_k=\frac{\log2}{-\log(1-2^{-k})}.$$ There are constants $C_k,c_k>0$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)\le C_kn\ell_k(n)\quad(n\ge k),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)\ge c_kn\quad\text{for all sufficiently large }n.$$ For every fixed $B>0$ there is $C_{k,B}<\infty$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})\le C_{k,B}n\ell_k(n)\quad(n\ge k).$$ If $B>U_k$, then also $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})\ge c_kn$ for all sufficiently large $n$, with the size cutoff allowed to depend on $k$ and $B$.
  - Explanation: For every fixed clause size at least three, the variance of the first unsatisfiable prefix index is bounded above by a constant multiple of the variable count times a factor that is logarithmic for size three and constant otherwise. It is bounded below by a positive multiple of the variable count. The same upper bound holds for any fixed linear cap, and the lower bound holds for caps beyond the first-moment density.
- **claim-one-clause-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-replacement)): For an integer $k\ge3$, let $$K_k=\bigl(2^k(k-1)\bigr)^{k-1}.$$ For every integer $u\ge k$, every $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$, and every integer $g\ge1$, $$\label{eq:one-replacement} R_g(S)\ge q_1(S)-K_k g^{-(k-1)}.$$
  - Explanation: A block of native clauses kills any assignment set with probability at least that of a single shorter clause, up to an additive error that decays as a fixed power of the block length. The error does not depend on the number of variables or on the set.
- **claim-sequential-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sequential-replacement)): For integers $k\ge3$ and $u\ge k$, every $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$, and all integers $d,g\ge1$, $$\label{eq:sequential-replacement} R_{dg}(S)\ge q_d(S)-dK_k g^{-(k-1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Replacing several shorter clauses one at a time by blocks of native clauses costs the one-clause error once per replacement, so the total loss is the number of replaced clauses times that error.
- **claim-lifetime-product** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-lifetime)): For an integer $k\ge3$, put $$\beta=\frac{k}{k-1},\qquad K_k=\bigl(2^k(k-1)\bigr)^{k-1},\qquad C_k=4(2K_k)^{1/(k-1)}.$$ For every integer $u\ge k$, every $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$, and every integer $d\ge1$, $$\label{eq:lifetime-product} \tau_k(S)q_d(S)^\beta\le C_k d^\beta.$$ Consequently, for nonempty $S$ and $0<a\le q_d(S)$, $$\label{eq:lifetime-level} \tau_k(S)\le C_k d^\beta a^{-\beta}.$$ There is no upper restriction on $d$ and no positive lower cutoff on $a$.
  - Explanation: The expected lifetime of any assignment set under native clauses times a power of its shorter-clause killing probability is bounded by a constant times a power of the number of shorter clauses. Consequently the lifetime is bounded at every positive level of the killing probability, with no cutoff and no restriction on the number of clauses.
- **claim-coordinate-omission** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coordinate-omission)): For the independent clause process above, $$\label{eq:coordinate-omission} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T)\le\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb ED_i^2.$$
  - Explanation: The variance of the capped first failure time is at most the sum over clause positions of the expected squared delay caused by omitting that clause. This is the coordinate-omission form of the Efron-Stein inequality, proved through orthogonal martingale differences.
- **claim-conditional-product-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-product)): Conditional on $\mathcal E$, the clauses at indices in $O$ are independent uniform proper $k$-clauses on the $u$ nonroots. The nonroot parts at indices in $Q$ are independent uniform proper $(k-1)$-clauses on the nonroots. These two collections are mutually independent.
  - Explanation: Given the exposed static information, the ordinary clauses are independent uniform native clauses on the nonroots, the hidden parts of test clauses are independent uniform shorter clauses, and the two families are independent of each other.
- **claim-root-flip-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-root-flip-tests)): For $i\le m<L$, on the event $\{\|J_m\|\ge1\}$, $$\label{eq:root-flip-tests} \mathbb P(A_m\mid\mathcal G_m) \le \mathbf1_{\{S_m\ne\varnothing\}}q_m^{\|J_m\|}.$$
  - Explanation: If restoring the omitted clause destroys satisfiability, every available root flip must fail, and the tests for distinct roots are independent. The conditional probability of the deletion event is therefore at most the killing probability raised to the number of available roots.
- **claim-weighted-delay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-delay)): Let $$W_m=\min\{L,\tau_k(S_m)+Z\}.$$ This is $\mathcal G_m$-measurable. For $i\le m<L$, on $A_m$, $$\label{eq:future-deletion-delay} \mathbb E\left[\sum_{\ell=m+1}^{L-1}\mathbf1_{A_\ell} \,\middle\|\,\mathcal F_m^*\right]\le W_m.$$ Consequently $$\label{eq:conditional-weighted-delay} \mathbb E[D_i^2\mid\mathcal E] \le3\sum_{m=i}^{L-1} \mathbb E[\mathbf1_{A_m}W_m\mid\mathcal E].$$
  - Explanation: On a deletion event the expected number of later deletion events is controlled by the weight combining the set lifetime and the incidence count. The second moment of the delay is then bounded by a constant multiple of a sum of deletion indicators times that weight.
- **claim-conditional-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-occupation)): There is a constant $C_k\ge1$, independent of the environment, such that for every $0<a\le1$, almost surely, $$\label{eq:conditional-occupation} \sum_{m=i}^{L-1} \mathbb P(S_m\ne\varnothing,\ q_m\ge a\mid\mathcal E) \le\min\{L,C_kd^\beta a^{-\beta}\}.$$
  - Explanation: The expected time the surviving assignment set spends above any given killing level is at most the cap and also at most a constant times a power of the incidence count and of the inverse level. Only the first visit is stopped on; later visits are charged to the remaining lifetime.
- **claim-integrated-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-integrated-occupation)): For $L\ge1$, almost surely and with a constant independent of the environment, $$\label{eq:integrated-occupation} \sum_{m=i}^{L-1} \mathbb E[\mathbf1_{\{S_m\ne\varnothing\}}q_m^\gamma\mid\mathcal E] \le \begin{cases} C_kd^\beta(1+\log L),&k=3,\\ C_kd^\beta,&k\ge4. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: Integrating the occupation bound over levels gives a bound that is constant in the cap for clause size at least four and grows logarithmically in the cap for size three, because the exponent integrability is borderline exactly at size three.
- **claim-conditional-squared-delay** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-conditional-squared-delay)): For $L\ge1$, set $$h_k(L)= \begin{cases} 1+\log L,&k=3,\\ 1,&k\ge4. \end{cases}$$ Then, almost surely, $$\label{eq:conditional-squared-delay} \mathbb E[D_i^2\mid\mathcal E] \le C_kd^{2\beta}h_k(L)\mathbf1_{\{V=0\}} +C_kLd^\beta\mathbf1_{\{V=1\}} +L^2\mathbf1_{\{V\ge2\}}.$$
  - Explanation: Conditionally on the static information, the expected squared delay is bounded in three regimes according to the number of collisions: no collision uses all root tests and the integrated occupation, one collision uses the remaining tests, and two or more collisions use the deterministic cap.
- **claim-weighted-root-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-root-counts)): Fix integers $k\ge3$ and $n\ge2k$, a real $B>0$, and a set of $k$ roots. Take $M$ independent uniform proper $k$-clauses on $n$ variables, where $M$ is a nonnegative integer with $M\le Bn$. Let $Z$ count clauses meeting a root, $V$ count clauses meeting at least two roots, and $d=\max\{1,Z\}$. For every fixed real $p\ge0$, $$\label{eq:incidence-moments} \mathbb Ed^p\le C_{k,B,p}.$$ For every fixed positive integer $r$, $$\label{eq:weighted-collision-moments} \mathbb E[d^p\mathbf1_{\{V\ge r\}}]\le C_{k,B,p,r}n^{-r}.$$
  - Explanation: Every fixed moment of the incidence count is bounded uniformly, and the moment weighted by the event of at least a given number of collisions decays as an inverse power of the number of variables with that exponent.
- **claim-cap-bridge** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cap-bridge)): For $b\ge a$, put $R_{a,b}=V_b-X_a+1$ and $D_a=H_n-X_a$. Then $$\begin{aligned} R_{a,b}&=\sum_{m=a}^{b}\mathbf 1_{\{H_n>m\}}, &0\le R_{a,b}&\le(b-a+1)\mathbf 1_{\{H_n>a\}},\label{eq:finite-cap-identity}\\ D_a&=(H_n-a)_+. \label{eq:uncapped-identity} \end{aligned}$$ For either $Z=R_{a,b}$ or $Z=D_a$, writing $\xi_{n,a}=2^nq_k^a$, $$\label{eq:cap-moments} \mathbb EZ\le 2^k\xi_{n,a},\qquad \mathbb EZ^2\le (2^{2k+1}-2^k)\xi_{n,a}.$$ If $X=V_b$ or $X=H_n$, respectively, then $$\begin{aligned} 0\le\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X)}-\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)} &\le 2^k\sqrt{1+q_k}\,\xi_{n,a}^{1/2}, \label{eq:cap-sigma}\\ 0\le\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X)-\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X_a) &\le\bigl[2a\,2^k+2^{2k+1}-2^k\bigr]\xi_{n,a}. \label{eq:cap-variance} \end{aligned}$$ The mean identities are $\mathbb EV_b=\mathbb EX_a-1+\mathbb ER_{a,b}$ and $\mathbb EH_n=\mathbb EX_a+\mathbb ED_a$.
  - Explanation: Finite-cap and infinite-cap statistics differ by remainders whose first and second moments are bounded by the exponentially small first-moment tail at the cap. This transfers variance and standard-deviation bounds between capped, last-satisfiable and uncapped versions, and gives exact mean identities.
- **claim-uncapped-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-uncapped)): For independent uniform proper clauses with fixed $k\ge3$, $$\label{eq:uncapped-variance} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)= \begin{cases} O(n\log n),&k=3,\\ O_k(n),&k\ge4. \end{cases}$$ The same upper bounds hold for the uncapped last satisfiable index $H_n-1$ and, for every fixed $B>0$, for $\min\{H_n-1,\lfloor Bn\rfloor\}$. Write $s_k(n)=\sqrt{n\ell_k(n)}$, with $\ell_k$ as in the introduction. For each of these statistics, or for $T_{n,B}$ from Theorem <a href="#thm:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:variance">1</a>, there is a constant $C$ (depending on fixed $k$ and, when present, $B$) such that $$\label{eq:mean-centered-concentration} \mathbb P\bigl(\|X-\mathbb EX\|\ge t s_k(n)\bigr)\le C t^{-2} \qquad(t>0)$$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: The variance upper bounds hold for the uncapped first failure time, the last satisfiable index and their linear caps. Chebyshev's inequality then gives concentration about the finite-size mean at the square-root fluctuation scale.
- **claim-sparse-satisfiability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-satisfiability)): For every real $0<c<e^{-2}$, every integer $k\ge3$, and every $n\ge k$, put $j=\lfloor cn\rfloor$. In the independent proper-clause model, $$\label{eq:sparse-satisfiability} 1-P_n(j)\le\frac{ec}{n^2}\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}t^2(e^2c)^t=O_c(n^{-2}).$$
  - Explanation: A prefix whose length is a small enough fraction of the variable count is unsatisfiable with probability decaying like an inverse square of the variable count. The proof uses a matching argument on the clause-variable incidence graph and Hall's theorem.
- **claim-variance-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-variance-lower)): Let $L_n$ be positive integer caps of order $O(n)$ and suppose $P_n(L_n-1)\le1/4$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\min\{H_n,L_n\})\ge c_k n$$ for all sufficiently large $n$, with $c_k>0$. In particular, for every fixed $B>U_k$ and every fixed $k\ge4$, $$\label{eq:sharp-variance} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)=\Theta_k(n),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})=\Theta_{k,B}(n),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\min\{H_n-1,\lfloor Bn\rfloor\})=\Theta_{k,B}(n).$$ For $k=3$, the corresponding variances lie between positive constant multiples of $n$ and $n\log n$.
  - Explanation: If the cap is linear and lies beyond the lower quantile level, the capped variance is at least a positive multiple of the variable count. This follows from the separation of central quantiles, kept at integer endpoints so atoms are allowed, and yields the sharp order for size at least four.
- **claim-lifetime-sharp** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-prop-lifetime-sharp)): Fix an integer $k\ge3$, and put $\beta=k/(k-1)$. For every real $\gamma<\beta$ and all constants $D,A>0$, there exist arbitrarily large integers $u\ge k$ and nonempty sets $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$ such that $$q_1(S)\ge A u^{-(k-1)},\qquad \tau_k(S)>Dq_1(S)^{-\gamma}.$$ Consequently, the killing-probability exponent $\beta$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:lifetime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:lifetime">4</a> cannot be decreased in a bound uniform over arbitrary assignment sets, even above any fixed multiple of the scale $u^{-(k-1)}$.
  - Explanation: Using subcubes with many frozen coordinates, the exponent in the lifetime bound cannot be lowered in any bound that is uniform over arbitrary assignment sets, even above a fixed multiple of the natural scale of the killing probability.
- **claim-replacement-sharp** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-prop-replacement-sharp)): Fix an integer $k\ge3$. For integers $g\ge2$, there are nonempty sets $S_g\subseteq\{0,1\}^{u_g}$, with $u_g\ge k$, for which $$q_1(S_g)-R_g(S_g)\sim 2^{-k}g^{-(k-1)} \qquad(g\to\infty).$$ In particular, no error $o(g^{-(k-1)})$, uniform over $u$ and the assignment set, can replace the error in Lemma <a href="#lem:one-replacement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:one-replacement">2</a>.
  - Explanation: On suitable frozen subcubes the deficit between one shorter clause and a block of native clauses is asymptotically a fixed constant times the inverse block length to the stated power, so no smaller uniform error is possible in the one-clause replacement lemma.
- **claim-center-convergence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cmp-convergence)): Fix $k\ge3$, $B>0$, and $0<\delta<1/2$. Suppose that there are constants $0<a\le A<B$ and numbers $\eta_n\ge0$ tending to zero such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{cmp:module-input} a\le e_n\le A,\qquad \mathbb P\bigl(\|T_n-\mu_n\|\ge n^{1-\delta}\bigr)\le\eta_n.$$ Then there is a finite constant $K\ge0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n=\min\{n_1,n_2\}$, with no restriction on $n_1/n_2$, $$\label{cmp:sum} e_{n_1+n_2}\ge\min\{e_{n_1},e_{n_2}\}-K n^{-\delta},$$ and, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{cmp:neighbor} e_{n+1}\ge e_n-K n^{-\delta}.$$ Moreover $e_n$ converges to a number $\alpha\in[a,A]$, and $$\label{cmp:strict-sides} P_n(\lfloor cn\rfloor)\longrightarrow \begin{cases}1,&0\le c<\alpha,\\0,&c>\alpha.\end{cases}$$ For every sufficiently large $s$ and every $N\ge s^2$, one has the one-sided estimate $$\label{cmp:tree-bound} e_N\ge e_s-K(1+B_\delta)s^{-\delta},\qquad B_\delta=\frac{(3/2)^\delta}{1-(2/3)^\delta}.$$
  - Explanation: Given bounded normalized means and a concentration estimate with a vanishing error, the normalized means satisfy approximate superadditivity and neighbor comparisons, converge to a limit, and the satisfiability probability tends to one below and zero above the limit. A one-sided tree estimate also holds.
- **claim-trial-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-trial-comparison)): Partition $N=n_1+n_2$ variables into blocks of sizes $n_1,n_2\ge k$, and put $w_j=n_j/N$ and $n=\min\{n_1,n_2\}$. Fix an integer $M\ge0$ and $0<\varepsilon<1$. Each of $M$ independent local trials chooses block $j$ with probability $w_j$ and inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause in that block. Each of $M$ independent diluted global trials inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause on all $N$ variables with probability $1-\varepsilon$, and inserts no clause otherwise. Let $Q_{\mathrm{loc}}$ and $Q_{\mathrm{dil}}$ be the respective probabilities that the resulting formulas are satisfiable. Then $$\begin{aligned} Q_{\mathrm{dil}} &\ge Q_{\mathrm{loc}}- C_k M\varepsilon^{-k}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\frac{w_j}{n_j^k}, & C_k&=\frac{[k(k-1)]^k}{2^k},\label{cmp:weighted-replacement}\\ Q_{\mathrm{dil}} &\ge Q_{\mathrm{loc}}-\frac{C_kM}{\varepsilon^k n^k}. \label{cmp:coarse-replacement} \end{aligned}$$ For $N=n+1$, if all local trials instead use a fixed main set of $n\ge k$ variables, then $$\label{cmp:neighbor-replacement} Q_{\mathrm{dil}}\ge Q_{\mathrm{loc}} -\frac{D_kM}{\varepsilon^k n^k},\qquad D_k=\frac{k^{2k}}{2^k}.$$ These statements impose no restriction on a block-size ratio.
  - Explanation: Local clause trials inside two variable blocks and diluted global trials on all variables give satisfiability probabilities that differ by an explicit loss, controlled by frozen-fraction bounds and uniform in the block-size ratio. A neighboring-size version holds with its own constant.
- **claim-prefix-variance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-prefix-variance)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for all integers $n\ge3$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_n)\le Cn(\log n)^4.$$ Consequently, for every $t>0$, $$\mathbb P\bigl(\|T_n-m_n\|\ge t\sqrt n(\log n)^2\bigr)\le Ct^{-2}.$$
  - Explanation: A second proof for clause size three, based on prefix exposure and incidence truncation, gives a weaker polylogarithmic factor in the variance bound and a corresponding Chebyshev tail bound about the finite-size mean.
- **claim-prefix-one-pair** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-one-pair)): For every integer $u\ge3$, every $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$, and every integer $g\ge1$, if the $g$ triples are independent uniform proper three-clauses on these $u$ variables, then $$\label{eq:prefix-one-pair} \mathbb P(\text{the \(g\) triples kill \(S\)}) \ge q_1(S)-64(g^{-2}+u^{-2}).$$
  - Explanation: For size three, a block of native clauses replaces one pair clause on a finite variable set with an explicit additive loss that depends on both the block length and the number of variables.
- **claim-prefix-lifetime** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-prefix-lifetime)): Let $K=64$. There is an absolute constant $K_1$ such that for every integer $u\ge3$, every nonempty $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$, every integer $d\ge1$, and every $a$ with $4Kd/u^2\le a\le1$, $$\label{eq:prefix-lifetime} q_d(S)\ge a\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \tau(S)\le K_1d^{3/2}a^{-3/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Above a finite-variable cutoff on the level, a lower bound on the pair-killing probability implies an upper bound on the expected three-clause lifetime of the set, with the same exponent as in the main argument.
- **claim-prefix-future-delay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-future-delay)): Set $\mathcal W_m=\min\{L,\tau(S_m)/p_0\}$. For $i\le m<L$, on $A_m$, $$\mathbb E\left[\sum_{\ell=m+1}^{L-1}\mathbf 1_{\{A_\ell\}} \,\middle\|\,\mathcal F_m\right]\le\mathcal W_m.$$ Consequently, $$\label{eq:prefix-weighted-delay} \mathbb ED_i^2\le3\sum_{m=i}^{L-1}\mathbb E[\mathbf 1_{\{A_m\}}\mathcal W_m].$$
  - Explanation: On a deletion event the expected number of later deletion events is at most the weight built from the set lifetime, and the second moment of the delay is bounded by a constant multiple of the weighted sum of deletion indicators.
- **claim-prefix-root-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-root-tests)): For every $i\le m<L$, on the $\mathcal G_m$-measurable event $\{\max_{j\in R}s_{j,m}\le d,\ \|J_m\|\ge1\}$, $$\label{eq:prefix-root-tests} \mathbb P(A_m\mid\mathcal G_m) \le\mathbf 1_{\{S_m\ne\varnothing\}}q_m^{\|J_m\|}.$$
  - Explanation: Under prefix conditioning, with a bounded number of tests per root and at least one available root, the deletion probability is at most the pair-killing probability raised to the number of available roots.
- **claim-prefix-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-counts)): Uniformly in $i\le m<L$ and in the fixed value of $C_i$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P\bigl(\max_{j\in R}s_{j,m}>d\bigr)&=O(n^{-4}), \label{eq:prefix-large-count}\\ \mathbb P(v_m\ge1)&=O(n^{-1}),\qquad \mathbb P(v_m\ge2)=O(n^{-2}). \label{eq:prefix-collision-counts} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Large per-root incidence counts are very rare and clauses meeting two roots occur with probabilities decaying as the first and second inverse powers of the variable count.
- **claim-prefix-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-reduction)): For all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly in $i$ and in the value of $C_i$, $$\label{eq:prefix-reduction} \mathbb ED_i^2\le C(1+d^{3/2})+ C d^{3/2}\sum_{m=i}^{L-1} \mathbb E\bigl[\mathbf 1_{\{S_m\ne\varnothing\}}q_m^{3/2}\bigr].$$
  - Explanation: The expected squared delay is reduced to a constant term plus a weighted sum of expected occupation of surviving sets, uniformly in the omitted index and in the omitted clause.
- **claim-prefix-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-occupation)): For all sufficiently large $n$ and every $a_0\le a\le1$, $$\label{eq:prefix-occupation} \sum_{m=i}^{L-1}\mathbb P(S_m\ne\varnothing,\ q_m\ge a) \le Cd^{3/2}a^{-3/2}.$$ For $0<a<a_0$, the same sum is at most $L$.
  - Explanation: Above the cutoff, the expected number of indices where the surviving set is nonempty at a given level is bounded by a power of the inverse level; below the cutoff the trivial bound by the cap is used.
- **claim-prefix-consequences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-prefix-consequences)): The variance and tail bounds of Theorem <a href="#thm:prefix-variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:prefix-variance">21</a> hold also for $W_n$ and $V_n$, each centered at its own expectation. Moreover $e_n\to\alpha_3$, the strict-side threshold of the proper three-clause model: for every fixed $c\ge0$ with $c\ne\alpha_3$, $$P_n(\lfloor cn\rfloor)\longrightarrow \begin{cases}1,&c<\alpha_3,\\0,&c>\alpha_3.\end{cases}$$ There is an absolute constant $C$ such that for all sufficiently large $n=\min\{n_1,n_2\}$ with $\max\{n_1,n_2\}\le3n$, and for all sufficiently large $n$ in the second inequality, $$\label{eq:prefix-center-comparisons} e_{n_1+n_2}\ge\min\{e_{n_1},e_{n_2}\}-Cn^{-1/4}, \qquad e_{n+1}\ge e_n-Cn^{-1/4}.$$
  - Explanation: The prefix-exposure variance and tail bounds transfer to the last-satisfiable statistics, the normalized means converge to the three-clause threshold with the stated strict-side limits, and comparable-size center comparisons hold with an explicit polynomial error.

      "conclusion": {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-root-counts",
        "exact_quote": "Fix integers $k\\ge3$ and $n\\ge2k$, a real $B>0$, and a set of $k$ roots. Take $M$ independent uniform proper $k$-clauses on $n$ variables, where $M$ is a nonnegative integer with $M\\le Bn$. Let $Z$ count clauses meeting a root, $V$ count clauses meeting at least two roots, and $d=\\max\\{1,Z\\}$. For every fixed real $p\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:incidence-moments}\n \\mathbb Ed^p\\le C_{k,B,p}.$$ For every fixed positive integer $r$, $$\\label{eq:weighted-collision-moments}\n \\mathbb E[d^p\\mathbf1_{\\{V\\ge r\\}}]\\le C_{k,B,p,r}n^{-r}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cap-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "For $b\\ge a$, put $R_{a,b}=V_b-X_a+1$ and $D_a=H_n-X_a$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n R_{a,b}&=\\sum_{m=a}^{b}\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_n>m\\}},\n &0\\le R_{a,b}&\\le(b-a+1)\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_n>a\\}},\\label{eq:finite-cap-identity}\\\\\n D_a&=(H_n-a)_+.\n \\label{eq:uncapped-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For either $Z=R_{a,b}$ or $Z=D_a$, writing $\\xi_{n,a}=2^nq_k^a$, $$\\label{eq:cap-moments}\n \\mathbb EZ\\le 2^k\\xi_{n,a},\\qquad\n \\mathbb EZ^2\\le (2^{2k+1}-2^k)\\xi_{n,a}.$$ If $X=V_b$ or $X=H_n$, respectively, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X)}-\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)}\n &\\le 2^k\\sqrt{1+q_k}\\,\\xi_{n,a}^{1/2},\n \\label{eq:cap-sigma}\\\\\n 0\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)\n &\\le\\bigl[2a\\,2^k+2^{2k+1}-2^k\\bigr]\\xi_{n,a}.\n \\label{eq:cap-variance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The mean identities are $\\mathbb EV_b=\\mathbb EX_a-1+\\mathbb ER_{a,b}$ and $\\mathbb EH_n=\\mathbb EX_a+\\mathbb ED_a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cap-bridge",
          "exact_quote": "For $b\\ge a$, put $R_{a,b}=V_b-X_a+1$ and $D_a=H_n-X_a$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n R_{a,b}&=\\sum_{m=a}^{b}\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_n>m\\}},\n &0\\le R_{a,b}&\\le(b-a+1)\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_n>a\\}},\\label{eq:finite-cap-identity}\\\\\n D_a&=(H_n-a)_+.\n \\label{eq:uncapped-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For either $Z=R_{a,b}$ or $Z=D_a$, writing $\\xi_{n,a}=2^nq_k^a$, $$\\label{eq:cap-moments}\n \\mathbb EZ\\le 2^k\\xi_{n,a},\\qquad\n \\mathbb EZ^2\\le (2^{2k+1}-2^k)\\xi_{n,a}.$$ If $X=V_b$ or $X=H_n$, respectively, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X)}-\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)}\n &\\le 2^k\\sqrt{1+q_k}\\,\\xi_{n,a}^{1/2},\n \\label{eq:cap-sigma}\\\\\n 0\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)\n &\\le\\bigl[2a\\,2^k+2^{2k+1}-2^k\\bigr]\\xi_{n,a}.\n \\label{eq:cap-variance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The mean identities are $\\mathbb EV_b=\\mathbb EX_a-1+\\mathbb ER_{a,b}$ and $\\mathbb EH_n=\\mathbb EX_a+\\mathbb ED_a$.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $b\\ge a$, put $R_{a,b}=V_b-X_a+1$ and $D_a=H_n-X_a$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n R_{a,b}&=\\sum_{m=a}^{b}\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_n>m\\}},\n &0\\le R_{a,b}&\\le(b-a+1)\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_n>a\\}},\\label{eq:finite-cap-identity}\\\\\n D_a&=(H_n-a)_+.\n \\label{eq:uncapped-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For either $Z=R_{a,b}$ or $Z=D_a$, writing $\\xi_{n,a}=2^nq_k^a$, $$\\label{eq:cap-moments}\n \\mathbb EZ\\le 2^k\\xi_{n,a},\\qquad\n \\mathbb EZ^2\\le (2^{2k+1}-2^k)\\xi_{n,a}.$$ If $X=V_b$ or $X=H_n$, respectively, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X)}-\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)}\n &\\le 2^k\\sqrt{1+q_k}\\,\\xi_{n,a}^{1/2},\n \\label{eq:cap-sigma}\\\\\n 0\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_a)\n &\\le\\bigl[2a\\,2^k+2^{2k+1}-2^k\\bigr]\\xi_{n,a}.\n \\label{eq:cap-variance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The mean identities are $\\mathbb EV_b=\\mathbb EX_a-1+\\mathbb ER_{a,b}$ and $\\mathbb EH_n=\\mathbb EX_a+\\mathbb ED_a$.",
      "explanation": "Finite-cap and infinite-cap statistics differ by remainders whose first and second moments are bounded by the exponentially small first-moment tail at the cap.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-uncapped",
        "exact_quote": "For independent uniform proper clauses with fixed $k\\ge3$, $$\\label{eq:uncapped-variance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)=\n \\begin{cases}\n O(n\\log n),&k=3,\\\\\n O_k(n),&k\\ge4.\n \\end{cases}$$ The same upper bounds hold for the uncapped last satisfiable index $H_n-1$ and, for every fixed $B>0$, for $\\min\\{H_n-1,\\lfloor Bn\\rfloor\\}$. Write $s_k(n)=\\sqrt{n\\ell_k(n)}$, with $\\ell_k$ as in the introduction. For each of these statistics, or for $T_{n,B}$ from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:variance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:variance\">1</a>, there is a constant $C$ (depending on fixed $k$ and, when present, $B$) such that $$\\label{eq:mean-centered-concentration}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(|X-\\mathbb EX|\\ge t s_k(n)\\bigr)\\le C t^{-2}\n \\qquad(t>0)$$ for all sufficiently large $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-uncapped",
          "exact_quote": "For independent uniform proper clauses with fixed $k\\ge3$, $$\\label{eq:uncapped-variance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)=\n \\begin{cases}\n O(n\\log n),&k=3,\\\\\n O_k(n),&k\\ge4.\n \\end{cases}$$ The same upper bounds hold for the uncapped last satisfiable index $H_n-1$ and, for every fixed $B>0$, for $\\min\\{H_n-1,\\lfloor Bn\\rfloor\\}$. Write $s_k(n)=\\sqrt{n\\ell_k(n)}$, with $\\ell_k$ as in the introduction. For each of these statistics, or for $T_{n,B}$ from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:variance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:variance\">1</a>, there is a constant $C$ (depending on fixed $k$ and, when present, $B$) such that $$\\label{eq:mean-centered-concentration}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(|X-\\mathbb EX|\\ge t s_k(n)\\bigr)\\le C t^{-2}\n \\qquad(t>0)$$ for all sufficiently large $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For independent uniform proper clauses with fixed $k\\ge3$, $$\\label{eq:uncapped-variance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)=\n \\begin{cases}\n O(n\\log n),&k=3,\\\\\n O_k(n),&k\\ge4.\n \\end{cases}$$ The same upper bounds hold for the uncapped last satisfiable index $H_n-1$ and, for every fixed $B>0$, for $\\min\\{H_n-1,\\lfloor Bn\\rfloor\\}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse-satisfiability",
        "exact_quote": "For every real $0<c<e^{-2}$, every integer $k\\ge3$, and every $n\\ge k$, put $j=\\lfloor cn\\rfloor$. In the independent proper-clause model, $$\\label{eq:sparse-satisfiability}\n 1-P_n(j)\\le\\frac{ec}{n^2}\\sum_{t=1}^{\\infty}t^2(e^2c)^t=O_c(n^{-2}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-variance-lower",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L_n$ be positive integer caps of order $O(n)$ and suppose $P_n(L_n-1)\\le1/4$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\min\\{H_n,L_n\\})\\ge c_k n$$ for all sufficiently large $n$, with $c_k>0$. In particular, for every fixed $B>U_k$ and every fixed $k\\ge4$, $$\\label{eq:sharp-variance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)=\\Theta_k(n),\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})=\\Theta_{k,B}(n),\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\min\\{H_n-1,\\lfloor Bn\\rfloor\\})=\\Theta_{k,B}(n).$$ For $k=3$, the corresponding variances lie between positive constant multiples of $n$ and $n\\log n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-variance-lower",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L_n$ be positive integer caps of order $O(n)$ and suppose $P_n(L_n-1)\\le1/4$ for all sufficiently large $n$.           "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $L_n$ be positive integer caps of order $O(n)$ and suppose $P_n(L_n-1)\\le1/4$ for all sufficiently large $n$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-lifetime-sharp",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k\\ge3$, and put $\\beta=k/(k-1)$. For every real $\\gamma<\\beta$ and all constants $D,A>0$, there exist arbitrarily large integers $u\\ge k$ and nonempty sets $S\\subseteq\\{0,1\\}^u$ such that $$q_1(S)\\ge A u^{-(k-1)},\\qquad\n \\tau_k(S)>Dq_1(S)^{-\\gamma}.$$ Consequently, the killing-probability exponent $\\beta$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:lifetime\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:lifetime\">4</a> cannot be decreased in a bound uniform over arbitrary assignment sets, even above any fixed multiple of the scale $u^{-(k-1)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-lifetime-sharp",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k\\ge3$, and put $\\beta=k/(k-1)$.           "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix an integer $k\\ge3$, and put $\\beta=k/(k-1)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-replacement-sharp",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k\\ge3$. For integers $g\\ge2$, there are nonempty sets $S_g\\subseteq\\{0,1\\}^{u_g}$, with $u_g\\ge k$, for which $$q_1(S_g)-R_g(S_g)\\sim 2^{-k}g^{-(k-1)}\n \\qquad(g\\to\\infty).$$ In particular, no error $o(g^{-(k-1)})$, uniform over $u$ and the assignment set, can replace the error in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:one-replacement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:one-replacement\">2</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-replacement-sharp",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k\\ge3$.           "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix an integer $k\\ge3$.       "explanation": "On suitable frozen subcubes the deficit between one shorter clause and a block of native clauses is asymptotically a fixed constant times the inverse block length to the stated power, so no smaller uniform error is possible in the one-clause replacement lemma.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cmp-convergence",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:proper-center-module\" label=\"thm:proper-center-module\"></span><span id=\"cmp:modular\" label=\"cmp:modular\"></span> <span id=\"cmp:centers\" label=\"cmp:centers\"></span>Fix $k\\ge3$, $B>0$, and $0<\\delta<1/2$. Suppose that there are constants $0<a\\le A<B$ and numbers $\\eta_n\\ge0$ tending to zero such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{cmp:module-input}\n a\\le e_n\\le A,\\qquad\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(|T_n-\\mu_n|\\ge n^{1-\\delta}\\bigr)\\le\\eta_n.$$ Then there is a finite constant $K\\ge0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n=\\min\\{n_1,n_2\\}$, with no restriction on $n_1/n_2$, $$\\label{cmp:sum}\n e_{n_1+n_2}\\ge\\min\\{e_{n_1},e_{n_2}\\}-K n^{-\\delta},$$ and, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{cmp:neighbor}\n e_{n+1}\\ge e_n-K n^{-\\delta}.$$ Moreover $e_n$ converges to a number $\\alpha\\in[a,A]$, and $$\\label{cmp:strict-sides}\n P_n(\\lfloor cn\\rfloor)\\longrightarrow\n \\begin{cases}1,&0\\le c<\\alpha,\\\\0,&c>\\alpha.\\end{cases}$$ For every sufficiently large $s$ and every $N\\ge s^2$, one has the one-sided estimate $$\\label{cmp:tree-bound}\n e_N\\ge e_s-K(1+B_\\delta)s^{-\\delta},\\qquad\n B_\\delta=\\frac{(3/2)^\\delta}{1-(2/3)^\\delta}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cmp-convergence",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:proper-center-module\" label=\"thm:proper-center-module\"></span><span id=\"cmp:modular\" label=\"cmp:modular\"></span> <span id=\"cmp:centers\" label=\"cmp:centers\"></span>Fix $k\\ge3$, $B>0$, and $0<\\delta<1/2$. Suppose that there are constants $0<a\\le A<B$ and numbers $\\eta_n\\ge0$ tending to zero such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{cmp:module-input}\n a\\le e_n\\le A,\\qquad\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(|T_n-\\mu_n|\\ge n^{1-\\delta}\\bigr)\\le\\eta_n.$$ Then there is a finite constant $K\\ge0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n=\\min\\{n_1,n_2\\}$, with no restriction on $n_1/n_2$, $$\\label{cmp:sum}\n e_{n_1+n_2}\\ge\\min\\{e_{n_1},e_{n_2}\\}-K n^{-\\delta},$$ and, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{cmp:neighbor}\n e_{n+1}\\ge e_n-K n^{-\\delta}.$$ Moreover $e_n$ converges to a number $\\alpha\\in[a,A]$, and $$\\label{cmp:strict-sides}\n P_n(\\lfloor cn\\rfloor)\\longrightarrow\n \\begin{cases}1,&0\\le c<\\alpha,\\\\0,&c>\\alpha.\\end{cases}$$ For every sufficiently large $s$ and every $N\\ge s^2$, one has the one-sided estimate $$\\label{cmp:tree-bound}\n e_N\\ge e_s-K(1+B_\\delta)s^{-\\delta},\\qquad\n B_\\delta=\\frac{(3/2)^\\delta}{1-(2/3)^\\delta}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"thm:proper-center-module\" label=\"thm:proper-center-module\"></span><span id=\"cmp:modular\" label=\"cmp:modular\"></span> <span id=\"cmp:centers\" label=\"cmp:centers\"></span>Fix $k\\ge3$, $B>0$, and $0<\\delta<1/2$. Suppose that there are constants $0<a\\le A<B$ and numbers $\\eta_n\\ge0$ tending to zero such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{cmp:module-input}\n a\\le e_n\\le A,\\qquad\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(|T_n-\\mu_n|\\ge n^{1-\\delta}\\bigr)\\le\\eta_n.$$ Then there is a finite constant $K\\ge0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n=\\min\\{n_1,n_2\\}$, with no restriction on $n_1/n_2$, $$\\label{cmp:sum}\n e_{n_1+n_2}\\ge\\min\\{e_{n_1},e_{n_2}\\}-K n^{-\\delta},$$ and, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{cmp:neighbor}\n e_{n+1}\\ge e_n-K n^{-\\delta}.$$ Moreover $e_n$ converges to a number $\\alpha\\in[a,A]$, and $$\\label{cmp:strict-sides}\n P_n(\\lfloor cn\\rfloor)\\longrightarrow\n \\begin{cases}1,&0\\le c<\\alpha,\\\\0,&c>\\alpha.\\end{cases}$$ For every sufficiently large $s$ and every $N\\ge s^2$, one has the one-sided estimate $$\\label{cmp:tree-bound}\n e_N\\ge e_s-K(1+B_\\delta)s^{-\\delta},\\qquad\n B_\\delta=\\frac{(3/2)^\\delta}{1-(2/3)^\\delta}.$$",
      "explanation": "Given bounded normalized means and a concentration estimate with a vanishing error, the normalized means satisfy approximate superadditivity and neighbor comparisons, converge to a limit, and the satisfiability probability tends to one below and zero above the limit. A one-sided tree estimate also holds.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cmp-convergence",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cmp-trial-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Partition $N=n_1+n_2$ variables into blocks of sizes $n_1,n_2\\ge k$, and put $w_j=n_j/N$ and $n=\\min\\{n_1,n_2\\}$. Fix an integer $M\\ge0$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$. Each of $M$ independent local trials chooses block $j$ with probability $w_j$ and inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause in that block. Each of $M$ independent diluted global trials inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause on all $N$ variables with probability $1-\\varepsilon$, and inserts no clause otherwise. Let $Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}$ be the respective probabilities that the resulting formulas are satisfiable. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\n &\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}-\n C_k M\\varepsilon^{-k}\\sum_{j=1}^{2}\\frac{w_j}{n_j^k},\n & C_k&=\\frac{[k(k-1)]^k}{2^k},\\label{cmp:weighted-replacement}\\\\\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\n &\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}-\\frac{C_kM}{\\varepsilon^k n^k}.\n \\label{cmp:coarse-replacement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $N=n+1$, if all local trials instead use a fixed main set of $n\\ge k$ variables, then $$\\label{cmp:neighbor-replacement}\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}\n       -\\frac{D_kM}{\\varepsilon^k n^k},\\qquad\n D_k=\\frac{k^{2k}}{2^k}.$$ These statements impose no restriction on a block-size ratio.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cmp-trial-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Partition $N=n_1+n_2$ variables into blocks of sizes $n_1,n_2\\ge k$, and put $w_j=n_j/N$ and $n=\\min\\{n_1,n_2\\}$. Fix an integer $M\\ge0$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$. Each of $M$ independent local trials chooses block $j$ with probability $w_j$ and inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause in that block. Each of $M$ independent diluted global trials inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause on all $N$ variables with probability $1-\\varepsilon$, and inserts no clause otherwise. Let $Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}$ be the respective probabilities that the resulting formulas are satisfiable. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\n &\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}-\n C_k M\\varepsilon^{-k}\\sum_{j=1}^{2}\\frac{w_j}{n_j^k},\n & C_k&=\\frac{[k(k-1)]^k}{2^k},\\label{cmp:weighted-replacement}\\\\\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\n &\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}-\\frac{C_kM}{\\varepsilon^k n^k}.\n \\label{cmp:coarse-replacement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $N=n+1$, if all local trials instead use a fixed main set of $n\\ge k$ variables, then $$\\label{cmp:neighbor-replacement}\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}\n       -\\frac{D_kM}{\\varepsilon^k n^k},\\qquad\n D_k=\\frac{k^{2k}}{2^k}.$$ These statements impose no restriction on a block-size ratio.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Partition $N=n_1+n_2$ variables into blocks of sizes $n_1,n_2\\ge k$, and put $w_j=n_j/N$ and $n=\\min\\{n_1,n_2\\}$. Fix an integer $M\\ge0$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$. Each of $M$ independent local trials chooses block $j$ with probability $w_j$ and inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause in that block. Each of $M$ independent diluted global trials inserts an independent uniform proper $k$-clause on all $N$ variables with probability $1-\\varepsilon$, and inserts no clause otherwise. Let $Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}$ be the respective probabilities that the resulting formulas are satisfiable. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\n &\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}-\n C_k M\\varepsilon^{-k}\\sum_{j=1}^{2}\\frac{w_j}{n_j^k},\n & C_k&=\\frac{[k(k-1)]^k}{2^k},\\label{cmp:weighted-replacement}\\\\\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\n &\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}-\\frac{C_kM}{\\varepsilon^k n^k}.\n \\label{cmp:coarse-replacement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $N=n+1$, if all local trials instead use a fixed main set of $n\\ge k$ variables, then $$\\label{cmp:neighbor-replacement}\n Q_{\\mathrm{dil}}\\ge Q_{\\mathrm{loc}}\n       -\\frac{D_kM}{\\varepsilon^k n^k},\\qquad\n D_k=\\frac{k^{2k}}{2^k}.$$ These statements impose no restriction on a block-size ratio.",
      "explanation": "Local clause trials inside two variable blocks and diluted global trials on all variables give satisfiability probabilities that differ by an explicit loss, controlled by frozen-fraction bounds and uniform in the block-size ratio.       "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cmp-trial-comparison",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **proper k-clause** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *proper $k$-clause* is a disjunction of literals on $k$ distinct variables.
- **capped first failure time** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a fixed $B>0$, the capped first failure time is $T_{n,B}=\min\{H_n,\lfloor Bn\rfloor\}$.
- **kills** ([source](#eq:set-survival)): A collection of clauses *kills* $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$ when no member of $S$ satisfies every clause in the collection.
- **proper s-clause on u variables** ([source](#eq:set-survival)): A proper $s$-clause, for $1\le s\le u$, chooses an $s$-element subset of the $u$ variables uniformly and then chooses independent fair signs on that subset.
- **frozen coordinate** ([source](#eq:frozen-killing)): For a nonempty set $S$, a coordinate is *frozen* if every member of $S$ has the same value there.
- **lifetime and shorter-clause killing probability** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $q_d(S)$ be the probability that $d$ independent proper $(k-1)$-clauses kill $S$, and let $\tau_k(S)$ be the expected number of independent proper $k$-clauses through its first kill.
- **roots of the omitted clause** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Call the variables of $C_i$ its roots, and fix them to the unique values that falsify $C_i$.
- **root type** ([source](#pm-h-9)): The *root type* of a clause records the subset of its variables in $R$ and the signs of their literals.
- **ordinary clause** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A clause is *ordinary* if that subset is empty.
- **test clause** ([source](#pm-h-9)): It is a *test clause* if the subset contains exactly one root and its literal is true at $t$.
- **collision** ([source](#pm-h-9)): We call a clause counted by $V$ a *collision*.
- **prefix-exposure roots** ([source](#pm-h-29)): Let $R$ be the three variables of $C_i$, called its *roots*, and let $t\in\{0,1\}^{R}$ be their unique values that falsify $C_i$.
- **prefix ordinary clause** ([source](#eq:prefix-future-mean)): A clause is *ordinary* if it uses only nonroots.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-variance-upper-bound | upper bound on the variance of the first unsatisfiable prefix index | ≤ | $C_kn\ell_k(n)$ | all n at least k |
| q-variance-lower-bound | lower bound on the variance of the first unsatisfiable prefix index | ≥ | $c_kn$ | all sufficiently large n, fixed k |
| q-first-moment-density | first-moment cap density | = | $\frac{\log2}{-\log(1-2^{-k})}$ | fixed k at least three |
| q-replacement-constant | one-clause replacement constant | = | $\bigl(2^k(k-1)\bigr)^{k-1}$ | fixed k at least three |
| q-replacement-error | block killing probability lower bound | ≥ | $q_1(S)-K_k g^{-(k-1)}$ | uniform in u, S and g |
| q-lifetime-product | lifetime times a power of the shorter-clause killing probability | ≤ | $C_k d^\beta$ | no restriction on d and no cutoff on the level |
| q-lifetime-constant | explicit lifetime constant | = | $4(2K_k)^{1/(k-1)}$ | fixed k at least three |
| q-lifetime-exponent | optimal lifetime exponent | = | $\beta=k/(k-1)$ | fixed k at least three |
| q-occupation-exponent | occupation exponent | = | $\frac{k(k-2)}{k-1}$ | fixed k at least three |
| q-available-root-tests | number of available root-flip tests | ≥ | $k-V$ | indices between the omitted index and the cap |
| q-conditional-occupation | expected occupation above a level | ≤ | $\min\{L,C_kd^\beta a^{-\beta}\}$ | almost surely, constant independent of the environment |
| q-incidence-probability | probability that one clause meets a root | ≤ | $\frac{k^2}{n}$ | n at least twice k |
| q-collision-probability | probability that one clause meets two roots | ≤ | $\frac{k^2(k-1)^2}{n^2}$ | n at least twice k |
| q-cap-first-moment | mean of a cap remainder | ≤ | $2^k\xi_{n,a}$ | either remainder |
| q-cap-variance-difference | variance difference between capped and uncapped statistics | ≤ | $\bigl[2a\,2^k+2^{2k+1}-2^k\bigr]\xi_{n,a}$ | integer cap a |
| q-sparse-unsatisfiability | unsatisfiability probability of a sparse prefix | ≤ | $\frac{ec}{n^2}\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}t^2(e^2c)^t$ | prefix length a small fraction of n |
| q-wilson-quantile-gap | separation of central quantiles | ≥ | $d_k\sqrt n$ | probability levels three quarters and one quarter, n beyond a threshold depending on k |
| q-poisson-repeat-mean | mean number of repeated-variable clauses in the Poisson auxiliary law | ≤ | $24$ | density between zero and eight |
| q-poisson-lower-constant | Poisson satisfiability probability lower bound | ≥ | $e^{-24}/2$ | densities below the normalized center minus a vanishing margin |
| q-replacement-deficit | asymptotic deficit between one shorter clause and a block | ~ | $2^{-k}g^{-(k-1)}$ | block length tending to infinity |
| q-trial-comparison-constant | weighted trial-comparison constant | = | $\frac{[k(k-1)]^k}{2^k}$ | no restriction on the block-size ratio |
| q-neighbor-comparison-constant | neighboring-size trial-comparison constant | = | $\frac{k^{2k}}{2^k}$ | fixed k at least three |
| q-center-upper-bound | upper bound on the normalized capped mean | = | $\begin{cases}7,&k=3,\\2^k+1,&k\ge4.\end{cases}$ | eventually in n |
| q-prefix-variance | prefix-exposure variance upper bound | ≤ | $Cn(\log n)^4$ | all n at least three |
| q-prefix-pair-loss | pair-to-triple replacement loss | ≤ | $64(g^{-2}+u^{-2})$ | uniform in S |
| q-prefix-cutoff | level cutoff in the prefix argument | = | $\frac{256d}{(n-3)^2}$ | sufficiently large n |
| q-prefix-lifetime | three-clause lifetime above the cutoff | ≤ | $K_1d^{3/2}a^{-3/2}$ | levels at or above the cutoff |
| q-prefix-concentration-error | concentration error for the center comparison | = | $C\frac{(\log n)^4}{\sqrt n}$ | n tending to infinity |
| q-prefix-comparison-loss | coarse trial-replacement loss for comparable sizes | ≤ | $756n^{-5/4}$ | large minimum block size |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-one-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-replacement): Case analysis on the number of frozen coordinates, independence of the clauses in the block, and a first-moment bound on the full cube in the borderline case. -> Uniform additive lower bound for the block killing probability.
- **step-sequential-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-sequential-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sequential-replacement): Replace the shorter clauses one at a time, conditioning on the other positions and applying the one-clause estimate, then telescope. -> Replacement bound for several shorter clauses.
- **step-lifetime** [claim](#stmt-prop-lifetime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lifetime): Choose a block length from the killing probability, split the native clause sequence into independent blocks and use a geometric first-success argument. -> Product bound relating lifetime and shorter-clause killing probability.
- **step-coordinate-omission** [claim](#stmt-lem-coordinate-omission) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coordinate-omission): Martingale difference decomposition over clause positions, conditional Jensen and orthogonality of increments. -> Variance bounded by the sum of expected squared delays.
- **step-conditional-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-product): Count the signed nonroot parts for a fixed root type and use independence of the original clauses. -> Product law for ordinary and test clauses given the static environment.
- **step-root-flip-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-root-flip-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root-flip-tests): If a root flip could succeed the deletion event fails; the tests for distinct roots are disjoint independent families of shorter clauses. -> Deletion probability bounded by a power of the shorter-clause killing probability.
- **step-weighted-delay** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-delay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-delay): Dominate the number of later deletion events by the chronological kill time, then expand the square and use the tower property. -> Second moment of the delay bounded by weighted deletion indicators.
- **step-conditional-occupation** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-occupation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-occupation): Stop at the first visit to the level and charge all later visits to the remaining lifetime of the nested set. -> Bound on the expected time spent above a level.
- **step-integrated-occupation** [claim](#stmt-lem-integrated-occupation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integrated-occupation): Layer-cake integral against the occupation bound, split at the crossover level for clause size three. -> Bounded integral for size at least four and logarithmic growth for size three.
- **step-squared-delay** [claim](#stmt-prop-conditional-squared-delay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-conditional-squared-delay): Split by the number of collisions: use all root tests, all but one, or the deterministic cap. -> Conditional second-moment bound in three collision regimes.
- **step-root-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-root-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-root-counts): Binomial incidence count with exponential moment, and a joint factorial-moment identity for collisions. -> Moment bounds for incidence and collision counts.
- **step-capped-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-root-counts-2): Average the conditional squared-delay bound over the environment using the moment bounds, then apply coordinate omission. -> Capped variance upper bound for every fixed cap multiple.
- **step-cap-bridge** [claim](#stmt-prop-cap-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cap-bridge): Integer tail-sum identities for the remainders, covariance with the capped variable and the triangle inequality in the square-integrable space. -> Variance and standard deviation comparison between capped and uncapped statistics.
- **step-uncapped-variance** [claim](#stmt-cor-uncapped) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-uncapped): Choose a late cap, transfer the capped bound through the cap comparison, use a Lipschitz contraction for the variance and apply Chebyshev's inequality. -> Uncapped variance bound and mean-centered concentration.
- **step-sparse-satisfiability** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-satisfiability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse-satisfiability): Hall's theorem on the clause-variable incidence graph and a union bound over deficient variable sets. -> Inverse-square bound on unsatisfiability of a sparse prefix.
- **step-variance-lower** [claim](#stmt-prop-variance-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-variance-lower): Convert the quantile separation into a second-moment bound through an independent copy and disjoint events. -> Linear variance lower bound and the sharp order for size at least four.
- **step-lifetime-sharp** [claim](#stmt-prop-lifetime-sharp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lifetime-sharp): Explicit frozen subcubes with geometric lifetime and a union bound for survival. -> Optimality of the lifetime exponent.
- **step-replacement-sharp** [claim](#stmt-prop-replacement-sharp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-replacement-sharp): Frozen subcubes and two-term inclusion-exclusion with a collision error. -> Optimality of the block-error exponent.
- **step-trial-comparison** [claim](#stmt-cmp-trial-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-trial-comparison): Frozen-fraction bounds on killing probabilities, Bernoulli's inequality and one-at-a-time replacement with telescoping. -> Loss bounds between local and diluted global trials.
- **step-center-convergence** [claim](#stmt-cmp-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-trial-comparison-2): Count transfer from concentration, trial comparison for two blocks and neighbors, then a deterministic balanced-comparison lemma. -> Convergence of normalized means and strict-side limits.
- **step-prefix-one-pair** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-one-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-one-pair): Frozen-variable formulas for pair and triple killing probabilities and a case split on the frozen count. -> Pair-to-triple replacement bound.
- **step-prefix-lifetime** [claim](#stmt-prop-prefix-lifetime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prefix-lifetime): Sequential replacement with the pair-to-triple bound and a geometric first-success argument over independent blocks. -> Lifetime bound above the cutoff.
- **step-prefix-future-delay** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-future-delay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-future-delay): Dominate later deletion events by the kill time of the nonroot set and expand the square. -> Weighted bound on the second moment of the delay.
- **step-prefix-root-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-root-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-root-tests): Root-flip argument under prefix conditioning with independent hidden pair clauses. -> Deletion probability bounded by a power of the pair-killing probability.
- **step-prefix-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-counts): Union bounds over roots, index choices and pairs of clauses. -> Rarity of large incidence counts and of collisions.
- **step-prefix-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-reduction): Discard rare environments, treat one collision with the weighted lifetime bound and zero collisions with all root tests. -> Reduction of the squared delay to an occupation sum.
- **step-prefix-occupation** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-occupation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-occupation): First-visit stopping time and the lifetime bound applied to the set at that time. -> Occupation bound above a level.
- **step-prefix-variance** [claim](#stmt-thm-prefix-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-occupation-2): Layer-cake integral of the occupation bound, then summation over omitted indices and Chebyshev's inequality. -> Polylogarithmic variance bound for clause size three.
- **step-prefix-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-prefix-consequences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-prefix-consequences): Transfer the variance bound through the cap identities, then apply the center comparison theorem using the sparse prefix lower bound. -> Variance for last-satisfiable statistics, convergence to the three-clause threshold and center comparisons.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-frozen-killing-probability**: For a nonempty set of assignments with b frozen coordinates among u, one uniformly chosen proper s-clause kills the set with probability (b)_s / (2^s (u)_s), where (a)_s is the falling factorial. This is zero when b < s.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-power-bound-below-k-frozen**: Applying the single-clause power lower bound for the k-clause killing probability in terms of the shorter-clause killing probability to a set with fewer than k frozen coordinates. -> The k-clause killing probability is exactly zero while the shorter-clause killing probability is positive, so no positive power lower bound holds.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The frozen-coordinate killing probability is an explicit finite formula. It can be computed exactly and checked against a brute-force enumeration of all signed clauses on small subcubes.

```python
# INPUTS: one line on stdin with three integers u b s (u variables, first b coordinates frozen at zero, clause size s).
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= s <= u, 0 <= b <= u, u small enough for the oracle enumeration.
# OUTPUTS: the exact killing probability of one proper s-clause as a reduced fraction p/q (or 0).
# TIME: O(s) exact integer operations.
# SPACE: O(1) big integers.
# ARITHMETIC: exact integers and Fraction, no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
u, b, s = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
num = 1
den = 1
for j in range(s):
    num *= (b - j)
    den *= (u - j)
print(Fraction(num, den*2**s))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves probabilistic variance bounds for the first unsatisfiable prefix of a random clause stream. It gives no routine, interface or finite procedure that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The clause process is analysed through martingale and omission arguments. The paper gives no simulation scheme, and nothing in it concerns game or physics engines. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No executable tool is described. The results are bounds on fluctuations of a hitting time, not a checker or generator. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lifetime and replacement estimates hold for every assignment set but are existence-style probabilistic inequalities. They do not describe a computational procedure with stated cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation of assignments or clauses is proposed for storage or querying. Sets of assignments appear only as analytic objects in the proofs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results contain no parallel or circuit-level construction, and nothing is stated about implementing the argument in hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper makes no hardness assumption, security reduction or adversary model. It studies fluctuations of a satisfiability hitting time only. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or generalisation statement appears. The bounds describe a random clause process, not a trained model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The satisfiability threshold fluctuation is a property of a random ensemble. The paper gives no objective, relaxation or solver guarantee that could drive an optimisation method. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages or fault models in the paper. The independent clauses are probabilistic objects, not distributed participants. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only external reading is statistical: the variance bounds give the width of the transition window for the uniform proper-clause model with replacement and fair signs. The paper states no bound on the finite-size bias of the mean, so this is not claimed as a usable construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-iid-proper-clause-model** (hypothesis): Every bound is proved only for independent uniform proper clauses on distinct variables with fair independent signs, where whole clauses may repeat. The symmetry used by the root-flip tests and the shorter-clause reduction depends on this law. Blocked use: ['Applying the variance bounds to structured or industrial formulas', 'Applying them to biased-sign, planted or monotone clause models']
- **limit-cap-lower-condition** (hypothesis): The linear lower bound on the capped variance needs the cap to lie beyond the lower quantile level. An early cap stops the process before its random transition and destroys the fluctuation. Blocked use: ['Claiming a linear variance lower bound for an arbitrary or too-early linear cap', 'Using the capped lower bound with a cap density at or below the first-moment density without checking the quantile condition']
- **limit-cap-equality-case** (edge_case): The exponential tail decay that removes the cap is obtained only for cap densities strictly above the first-moment density. The boundary case is not covered by the argument. Blocked use: ['Removing the cap at density exactly equal to the first-moment density using this exponential tail argument']
- **limit-three-clause-log-gap** (approximation): For clause size three the upper bound keeps a logarithmic factor, so this paper gives no matching two-sided linear order there. The sharper order comes from a companion result, not from this argument. Blocked use: ['Quoting a linear-order variance for clause size three as proved by this argument', 'Treating the logarithmic factor as removable within this proof']
- **limit-no-bias-rate** (formal_scope): The concentration statements are centred at the finite-size expectation. The limiting threshold identifies only the limit of the normalized mean and supplies no rate for the offset of the mean at the fluctuation scale. Blocked use: ['Predicting the finite-size mean offset from the limiting threshold times n', 'Centring the transition window at the limiting threshold times n']
- **limit-frozen-formula-stage** (edge_case): The single-clause killing probability from frozen coordinates vanishes when fewer than k coordinates are frozen, even if the shorter-clause killing probability is positive. A positive-power lower bound relating the two therefore fails for sets with few frozen coordinates. Blocked use: ['Using a single native clause as a substitute for a shorter clause on sets with few frozen coordinates', 'Treating the killing-probability formula as nonzero without checking the frozen count']
- **limit-uniform-sharpness-only** (counterexample_scope): The sharpness constructions show the exponents are optimal only for estimates uniform over arbitrary assignment sets. They do not rule out better lifetime estimates for structured classes of sets, and they do not show the logarithm for clause size three is necessary. Blocked use: ['Concluding that the logarithm in the three-clause upper bound cannot be removed from the sharpness results', 'Concluding that no better lifetime exponent exists for structured assignment sets']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 235 (`lean/docs/235.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A Limiting Satisfiability Threshold for Every Fixed Clause Size](../../preprints/A-Limiting-Satisfiability-Threshold-for-Every-Fixed-Clause-Size-September-25-2026/article.pdf)
> - [Variance of the Random $k$-SAT Hitting Time](../../preprints/Variance-of-the-Random-k-SAT-Hitting-Time-September-27-2026/article.pdf)
> - [Computing the Random 3-SAT Threshold](../../preprints/Computing-the-Random-3-SAT-Threshold-September-27-2026/article.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The random $k$-SAT threshold problem asks whether satisfiability changes at one limiting clause density. The formalization proves that every fixed integer $k\ge3$ has a finite positive threshold $\alpha_k$: for every fixed density $c<\alpha_k$, the satisfiability probability tends to one as the number of variables grows, while for every $c>\alpha_k$ it tends to zero. Clauses use distinct variables with independent uniform signs and are sampled independently with replacement. The statement makes no assertion at the threshold itself.
>
> Let $H_n$ be the first unsatisfiable prefix length in random $k$-SAT with independent uniformly signed clauses on $k$ distinct variables, sampled with replacement. The formalization proves $\mathrm{Var}(H_n)=\Theta_k(n)$ for every fixed $k\ge4$. For $k=3$, it proves a positive linear lower bound and an $O(n\log n)$ upper bound. The same upper bounds hold after clipping at any fixed positive multiple of $n$, and linear lower bounds hold for sufficiently high clipping levels.
>
> The linked supplements prove sharpness of the separate survival-lifetime and clause-replacement estimates used in the argument. They do not show that the logarithmic factor in the $k=3$ variance bound is necessary.
>
> The formalization proves that the limiting threshold $\alpha$ for uniform random $3$-SAT is a computable positive real. Satisfiability tends to one at every fixed density below $\alpha$ and to zero at every fixed density above it. One deterministic machine produces a rational number $q_r$ for every requested precision $r$ with $|q_r-\alpha|\le2^{-r}$. No computable rate of finite-size convergence is assumed in the statement.

Comparator source `SATVariance.lean` (2226 bytes, sha256 `8f93bd2b1d89a67b7c19ed9e52d713820344eaa8db1e7d811ed63437bc5b6f3a`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory

namespace RandomKSAT

abbrev Assignment (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Bool

def Clause (n k : ℕ) :=
  (s : {s : Finset (Fin n) // s.card = k}) × (s.1 → Bool)

instance clauseFintype (n k : ℕ) : Fintype (Clause n k) := by
  classical
  unfold Clause
  infer_instance

instance clauseMeasurableSpace (n k : ℕ) : MeasurableSpace (Clause n k) := ⊤

instance clauseMeasurableSingletonClass (n k : ℕ) :
    MeasurableSingletonClass (Clause n k) := ⟨fun _ => trivial⟩

def Satisfies {n k : ℕ} (c : Clause n k) (a : Assignment n) : Prop :=
  ∃ v : c.1.1, a v = c.2 v

abbrev Stream (n k : ℕ) := ℕ → Clause n k

def clauseLaw (n k : ℕ) : Measure (Clause n k) := uniformOn Set.univ

def streamLaw (n k : ℕ) : Measure (Stream n k) :=
  Measure.infinitePi (fun _ : ℕ => clauseLaw n k)

def PrefixSAT {n k : ℕ} (ω : Stream n k) (m : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ a : Assignment n, ∀ i < m, Satisfies (ω i) a

def firstFailure {n k : ℕ} (ω : Stream n k) : ℕ∞ :=
  sInf ((fun m : ℕ => (m : ℕ∞)) '' {m | ¬ PrefixSAT ω m})

def H {n k : ℕ} (ω : Stream n k) : ℝ := (firstFailure ω).toNat

def cap (n : ℕ) (B : ℝ) : ℕ := ⌊B * n⌋₊

def T {n k : ℕ} (B : ℝ) (ω : Stream n k) : ℝ :=
  (min (firstFailure ω) (cap n B : ℕ∞)).toNat

def ell (k n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if k = 3 then Real.log (Real.exp 1 * n) else 1

def U (k : ℕ) : ℝ := Real.log 2 / (-Real.log (1 - ((2 : ℝ) ^ k)⁻¹))

theorem variance_main (k : ℕ) (hk : 3 ≤ k) :
    (∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧ ∀ n : ℕ, k ≤ n →
      variance (H : Stream n k → ℝ) (streamLaw n k) ≤ C * n * ell k n) ∧
    (∀ B : ℝ, 0 < B → ∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧ ∀ n : ℕ, k ≤ n →
      variance (T B : Stream n k → ℝ) (streamLaw n k) ≤ C * n * ell k n) ∧
    (∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧
      (∃ N : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, N ≤ n → k ≤ n →
        c * n ≤ variance (H : Stream n k → ℝ) (streamLaw n k)) ∧
      (∀ B : ℝ, U k < B → ∃ N : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, N ≤ n → k ≤ n →
        c * n ≤ variance (T B : Stream n k → ℝ) (streamLaw n k))) := by
  sorry

end RandomKSAT

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>H</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> be the index of the first unsatisfiable prefix in random <i>k</i>-SAT on <i>n</i> variables, with independent uniformly signed clauses using <i>k</i> distinct variables and sampled with replacement. For every fixed <i>k</i> ≥ 4, we prove $`\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\nolimits (H_n)=\Theta_k(n)`$. For <i>k</i> = 3, the variance is bounded below by a positive multiple of <i>n</i> and above by a constant multiple of $`n\log n`$; the companion paper on random 3-SAT sharpens this to $`\Theta(n)`$. The same upper bounds proved here hold after clipping at any fixed positive multiple of <i>n</i>.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Fluctuations of the satisfiability transition

Add independent random clauses to a Boolean formula until the formula becomes unsatisfiable. How much does the number of clauses at this first failure vary from one sample to another? For every fixed clause size $k\ge4$, we prove that its variance has order $n$, where $n$ is the number of variables. For three-literal clauses the variance lies between positive constant multiples of $n$ and $n\log n$.

A *proper $k$-clause* is a disjunction of literals on $k$ distinct variables. Fix $k\ge3$ and $n\ge k$, and let $C_1,C_2,\ldots$ be independent uniform choices from the $2^k\binom nk$ proper signed clauses on $n$ variables. Signs are independent and fair; whole clauses may repeat. Put $$F_m=C_1\wedge\cdots\wedge C_m,\qquad F_0=\mathrm{true},\qquad
 H_n=\min\{m\ge1:F_m\text{ is unsatisfiable}\}.$$ For a fixed $B>0$, the capped first failure time is $T_{n,B}=\min\{H_n,\lfloor Bn\rfloor\}$. Each assignment survives a clause with probability $1-2^{-k}$, so <a id="eq:intro-first-moment"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-first-moment}
 \Pr(H_n>m)\le2^n(1-2^{-k})^m.$$ Thus $H_n$ is almost surely finite and has finite moments.

<a id="stmt-thm-variance"></a>
<div id="thm:variance" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Fix an integer $k\ge3$, and write $$\ell_k(n)=\begin{cases}\log(en),&k=3,\\1,&k\ge4,\end{cases}
 \qquad U_k=\frac{\log2}{-\log(1-2^{-k})}.$$ There are constants $C_k,c_k>0$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)\le C_kn\ell_k(n)\quad(n\ge k),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)\ge c_kn\quad\text{for all sufficiently large }n.$$ For every fixed $B>0$ there is $C_{k,B}<\infty$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})\le C_{k,B}n\ell_k(n)\quad(n\ge k).$$ If $B>U_k$, then also $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})\ge c_kn$ for all sufficiently large $n$, with the size cutoff allowed to depend on $k$ and $B$.*

</div>

All constants concern fixed $k$. The lower condition on the cap is substantive: an early cap can stop the process before its random transition. In the proof, it is enough to have integer caps $L_n=O(n)$ with $\Pr(H_n>L_n-1)\le1/4$ eventually; $B>U_k$ is an explicit sufficient condition. This argument leaves a logarithmic gap for three-literal clauses; the companion paper (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[LinearThreeSATVariance](#ref-LinearThreeSATVariance)] closes it and proves $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(H_n)=\Theta(n)$ for the uncapped proper 3-clause process.

The identity $\Pr(F_m\text{ is satisfiable})=\Pr(H_n>m)$ turns these variance bounds into bounds on the transition window. Between any fixed probability levels $\eta$ and $1-\eta$, $0<\eta<1/2$, its width is $O_{k,\eta}(\sqrt{n\ell_k(n)})$. Chebyshev’s inequality also gives $$\Pr\bigl(|H_n-\mathbb EH_n|\ge t\sqrt{n\ell_k(n)}\bigr)
 \le C_kt^{-2}\qquad(t>0).$$ This is concentration about the finite-size expectation. The limiting threshold $\alpha_k$ of (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[FixedKThreshold](#ref-FixedKThreshold)] identifies $\lim_n\mathbb EH_n/n$, but supplies no bound on the bias $\mathbb EH_n-\alpha_kn$ at the fluctuation scale above. For $k=3$, the separate companion (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[ComputableThreeSATThreshold](#ref-ComputableThreeSATThreshold)] proves that the limiting threshold $\alpha_3$ is a computable real.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier work and the new estimate

The location and the width of a threshold are different questions. Friedgut (Friedgut 1999, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Friedgut](#ref-Friedgut)] proved a sharp threshold sequence for each fixed clause size, without asserting convergence of that sequence. Achlioptas and Peres (Achlioptas and Peres 2004, Theorems 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[AchlioptasPeres](#ref-AchlioptasPeres)] used a weighted second moment to locate the threshold within $O(k)$ of $2^k\log2$ as $k$ grows. Ding, Sly, and Sun (Ding, Sly, and Sun 2022, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[DSS](#ref-DSS)] proved the predicted limiting threshold for all sufficiently large $k$. Carenini’s paper (Carenini 2026a, Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Carenini2026Polynomial](#ref-Carenini2026Polynomial)], made public on October 5, 2026, establishes the satisfiability conjecture for every fixed $k\ge3$. We credit Carenini with priority for the resolution of the satisfiability conjecture. The companion (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[FixedKThreshold](#ref-FixedKThreshold)] establishes a limiting location for every fixed $k\ge3$; its independent forcing argument gives the variance bound $O_k(n^{1+2/k})$ for the capped last-satisfiable prefix $\min(H_n-1,2^{k+1}n)$ (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[FixedKThreshold](#ref-FixedKThreshold)].

For transition width, Wilson (Wilson 2002, Corollary 4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Wilson](#ref-Wilson)] proved an $\Omega_k(\sqrt n)$ separation of central quantiles in the independent proper-clause model. We convert that separation into the variance lower bound, keeping the integer endpoints so the argument allows atoms. Carenini (Carenini 2026b, Corollaries 7.10–7.11) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Carenini](#ref-Carenini)] proves an $O_{k,\eta}(n/\log n)$ window upper bound for fixed $k\ge3$, including proper independent clauses. Her subsequent paper gives the polynomial window bound $O_{k,\eta}(n^{1/2+1/k})$ and the hitting-time variance bound $O_k(n^{1+2/k})$ (Carenini 2026a, Theorems 1.1 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Carenini2026Polynomial](#ref-Carenini2026Polynomial)]; the window estimate, together with the Abbe–Montanari criterion, yields the limiting-threshold theorem credited above. The upper bounds here sharpen these fluctuation estimates. For $k=3$, the companion (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[LinearThreeSATVariance](#ref-LinearThreeSATVariance)] further removes the logarithmic loss in this paper’s upper bound.

Our upper bound uses the coordinate-omission form of the Efron–Stein inequality (Efron and Stein 1981; Boucheron et al. 2005) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[EfronStein](#ref-EfronStein), [BBLM](#ref-BBLM)]. Deleting one clause leaves its $k$ variables available for root-flip tests of the remaining solutions. When no other clause meets two of these variables, the $k$ tests are independent and use clauses of length $k-1$; we account separately for such collisions. A set of assignments that is easily killed by those shorter clauses must also have a short expected lifetime under $k$-clauses. The replacement of a shorter constraint by a block of native clauses has a qualitative predecessor in Friedgut’s half-cube argument (Friedgut 1999, Lemma 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Friedgut](#ref-Friedgut)]; sequential conditioning on a residual assignment set is developed in Carenini (Carenini 2026b, Theorem 4.3 and Lemma 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Carenini](#ref-Carenini)]. We prove a block estimate uniform in the number of variables and the block length. It also covers assignment sets that no single $k$-clause can kill.

The main improvement comes after this replacement step. Instead of bounding the number of clauses incident to the deleted clause’s variables by a logarithmic cutoff, we retain that number as a random parameter. Its fixed moments are bounded. The proof must therefore use the incidence count without destroying the independence of the shorter-clause tests or of future ordinary clauses. Conditioning on the incidence pattern through the cap achieves both. Collisions involving two of the deleted clause’s variables are paid for by joint incidence and collision moments.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The route through the upper bound

Write $L=\lfloor Bn\rfloor$. If deleting $C_i$ while preserving the other clause indices delays the capped first failure by $D_i$, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(T_{n,B})\le\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb ED_i^2.$$ The delay $D_i$ counts the prefixes at which restoring $C_i$ destroys satisfiability, so $D_i^2$ counts ordered pairs of such prefixes. The problem is to control this occupation time.

Here is the shorter-clause estimate used for that purpose. A collection of clauses *kills* $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^u$ if no member of $S$ satisfies every clause. Let $q_d(S)$ be the probability that $d$ independent proper $(k-1)$-clauses kill $S$, and let $\tau_k(S)$ be the expected number of independent proper $k$-clauses through its first kill. For $u\ge k$, $d\ge1$, and $\beta=k/(k-1)$, we prove in Section <a href="#sec:replacement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:replacement">2</a> that $$\tau_k(S)q_d(S)^\beta\le C_kd^\beta.$$ This estimate holds for every assignment set, with no lower cutoff on $q_d(S)$.

Section <a href="#sec:deletion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:deletion">3</a> connects this statement to clause deletion. Call the variables of $C_i$ its roots, and fix them to the unique values that falsify $C_i$. Let $S_m$ be the nonroot parts of solutions of the prefix with $C_i$ omitted and the roots fixed this way. A clause disjoint from the roots is an ordinary $k$-clause on the nonroots. A clause meeting one root and satisfied by that root hides a $(k-1)$-clause: its nonroot part becomes a constraint when that root is flipped. If restoring $C_i$ destroys satisfiability, each allowed root flip must fail. In the absence of collisions this gives $k$ independent tests killing $S_m$.

Conditional on the exposed incidence pattern, let $Z$ be the number of clauses meeting the roots and put $d=\max\{1,Z\}$. The lifetime estimate bounds both the expected remaining duration of a deletion event and the total time spent at levels $q_d(S_m)\ge a$. Write $q=q_d(S_m)$. The $k$ independent tests contribute $q^k$, while the expected remaining duration costs a factor $q^{-\beta}$. Integrating the resulting $q^{k-\beta}$ over time costs a second lifetime factor: the expected occupation above level $a$ is bounded by a constant times $a^{-\beta}$. The near-zero part of the resulting integral is therefore $$\int a^{\,k-2\beta-1}\,da.$$ At $k=3$, $k=2\beta$ and the finite cap produces a logarithm; for $k\ge4$, $k>2\beta$ and the integral is bounded. Section <a href="#sec:occupation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:occupation">4</a> then averages the root counts and proves the capped upper bound. Exponential tails remove a sufficiently late cap. Wilson’s quantile bound supplies the separate lower-bound argument.

Section <a href="#sec:interfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:interfaces">5</a> removes a sufficiently late cap and records the exact first-unsatisfiable and last-satisfiable endpoint identities. Section <a href="#sec:lower-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lower-bounds">6</a> proves the lower bound, completing the variance theorem.
